{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{25.37901pt}{-1.79169pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{{}}{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{}}{}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{58.58308pt}{-1.79169pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{{}}{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{}}{}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{94.63243pt}{-2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right),

\displaystyle\left(z_{m}(x)\right)_{j,l}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}x_{j,m+l}&\text{for all $l\in\mathbb{N}$}.\end{cases}

This map is an approximation of (
66
). Notice that for the initial data
f_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
,
f_{0}\circ z_{m}
contains
4^{m}
scaled copies of
f_{0}
, one on each tile in the square lattice with widths
2^{-m}
.

For any
r,r^{\prime}\in\{1,...,2^{k-1}\}
, define the intervals
J=[(r-1)2^{1-k},r2^{1-k}]
,
J^{\prime}=[(r^{\prime}-1)2^{1-k},r^{\prime}2^{1-k}]
. Then
z_{m}
is a bijection from this tile
J\times J^{\prime}
to
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, that is

z_{m}:J\times J^{\prime}\leftrightarrow\mathbb{T}^{2}.

Moreover, for
d\mu
the Lebesgue-measure on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
\left.z_{m}\right|_{J\times J^{\prime}}\circ d\mu=4^{-m}d\mu
. In particular
z_{m}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\mathbb{T}^{2}
is measure preserving, and

\int_{J\times J^{\prime}}f_{0}\circ z_{m}\;dx=\frac{1}{4^{m}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}f_{0}\;dx.

Therefore, we deduce that

(67)

f_{0}\circ z_{m}\xrightharpoonup{m\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}f_{0}(y)\;dy,

with the above convergence in weak-
*

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.

By (
59
), for all
K\in\mathbb{N}
,
m\in\{1,...,L\}
, we may write
f^{K}(\cdot,T_{m})=f_{0}\circ\big(\tilde{y}_{T_{m}}^{K}\big)^{-1}
. We now wish to approximate
f^{K}(\cdot,T_{m})
by
f_{0}\circ z_{m}
to prove the mixing formula (
58
).

Fix some
\epsilon>0
. Take a smooth approximation
\phi_{\epsilon}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
of
f_{0}
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, so that
\left\|\phi_{\epsilon}-f_{0}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\leq\epsilon
. Since
\big(\tilde{y}_{T_{m}}^{K}\big)^{-1}
,
z_{m}
are measure preserving, we see that also

(68)

\begin{gathered}\left\|\phi_{\epsilon}\circ\big(\tilde{y}_{T_{m}}^{K}\big)^{-1}-f_{0}\circ\big(\tilde{y}_{T_{m}}^{K}\big)^{-1}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\leq\epsilon,\\
\left\|\phi_{\epsilon}\circ z_{m}-f_{0}\circ z_{m}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\leq\epsilon.\end{gathered}

Next, consider the square lattice with widths
2^{m-K-1}
, that is for
r,r^{\prime}\in\{1,...,2^{1+K-m}\}
define
J=[(r-1)2^{m-K-1},r2^{m-K-1}]
, and
J^{\prime}=[(r-1)2^{m-K-1},r2^{m-K-1}]
.

Then, by (
66
), we see that for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
\big(\tilde{y}_{T_{m}}^{K}\big)^{-1}(x)\in J\times J^{\prime}
if and only if
z_{m}(x)\in J\times J^{\prime}
. But then, by the Lipschitz bound on
\phi_{\epsilon}
,

\left|\phi_{\epsilon}\circ\big(\tilde{y}_{T_{m}}^{K}\big)^{-1}(x)-\phi_{\epsilon}\circ z_{m}(x)\right|\leq\sqrt{2}\left\|\nabla\phi_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}2^{m-K-1}.

Therefore,

\left\|\phi_{\epsilon}\circ\big(\tilde{y}_{T_{m}}^{K}\big)^{-1}-\phi_{\epsilon}\circ z_{m}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\leq\sqrt{2}\left\|\nabla\phi_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}2^{m-K-1}.

In light of (
68
), we deduce that

(69)

\left\|f^{K}(\cdot,T_{m})-f_{0}\circ z_{m}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\leq 2\epsilon+\sqrt{2}\left\|\nabla\phi_{\epsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}2^{m-K-1}.

Recall that
f^{K}\xrightarrow{K\to\infty}f^{\infty}
strongly in
C^{0}([0,42-\epsilon];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
for all
\epsilon>0
. Therefore, for
m\in\mathbb{N}
fixed,
f^{K}(\cdot,T_{m})\xrightarrow{K\to\infty}f^{\infty}(\cdot,T_{m})
strongly in
L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.

So, by (
69
), for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we see that
f^{\infty}(\cdot,T_{m})=f_{0}\circ z_{m}
. That is
f^{\infty}(\cdot,T_{m})
contains
4^{m}
scaled copies of
f_{0}
, one on each tile in the square lattice with widths
2^{-m}
.

Hence, by (
67
),
f^{\infty}(\cdot,T_{m})\xrightharpoonup{m\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}f_{0}(y)\;dy
converges in weak-
*

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.

Since
f^{\infty}\in C_{\mathrm{weak-}*}^{0}L^{\infty}
, with
f^{\infty}(\cdot,t)
independent of
t\in[42,50]
, and
T_{m}\xrightarrow{m\to\infty}42
, we have proved the mixing formula (
58
).
∎

Finally, it remains to find a suitably fast-growing sequence
\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}}\subset\mathbb{N}
in Definition
6.5
. Then to apply Theorem
4.3
to the sequence of vector fields
u_{\mathcal{K}}^{\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N_{K}}}}
, indexed by
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{N}
. This will allow us to approximate the vanishing viscosity limit to (

ν

-ADE
) along
u_{\infty}^{\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}}}
. Additionally, by subsequently time-reversing the vector field
u_{\infty}^{\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}}}
on the time interval
[50,100]
, we will obtain the inadmissible behaviour below.

Theorem 6.7

(Inadmissible vanishing viscosity limit)
.

There exists a divergence-free vector field
u\in L^{\infty}([0,100];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, such that for any initial data
f_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, and for
f^{\nu}
the unique solution to (

ν

-ADE
) along
u
with initial data
f_{0}
, one has

f^{\nu}\xrightarrow{\nu\to 0}f,

with the above convergence in weak-
*

L^{\infty}([0,100];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
, strong in
L^{p}([0,42];L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
,
C^{0}([0,42-\epsilon];L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
,
L^{p}([58,100];L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
, and
C^{0}([58+\epsilon,100];L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
for all
p\in[1,\infty)
, and all
\epsilon>0
. The limit function
f\in C_{\mathrm{weak-}*}^{0}([0,100];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
is a weak solution to (
TE
) along
u
with initial data
f_{0}
.

Moreover, for all
t\in[42,58]

f(\cdot,t)\equiv\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}f_{0}(y)\;dy,

is perfectly mixed to its spatial average.

Furthermore, for all
t\in[0,100]
,
f(\cdot,t)=f(\cdot,100-t)
and in particular,

f(\cdot,100)=f_{0},

is perfectly unmixed. In particular, if
f_{0}
is not constant, any
L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
norms of
f(\cdot,t)
(for
p\in(1,\infty]
) increase after
t=58
, contrary to the entropy-admissibility criterion in
[
19
]
.

Proof.

Recall the language and notation introduced in Definitions
6.4
,
6.5
, as well as Definitions
2.4
,
2.6
of renormalised and Lagrangian solutions to (
TE
).

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
we denote by
\mathcal{K}_{n}\in\mathcal{N}
the isomorphism between the well orders
(\mathbb{N},<)
and
(\mathcal{N},<_{\mathrm{lex}})
. That is
n\mapsto\mathcal{K}_{n}
is a bijection from
\mathbb{N}
to
\mathcal{N}
, and for all
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that
n_{1}<n_{2}
if and only if
\mathcal{K}_{n_{1}}<_{\mathrm{lex}}\mathcal{K}_{n_{2}}
.

For an infinite sequence
\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}}\subset\mathbb{N}
with
L_{(k,m,i,q)}\geq k+1
for all
(k,m,i,q)\in\mathcal{N}
, we define
u_{n}:\mathbb{T}^{2}\times[0,100]\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
by

(70)

u_{n}(x,t)=\begin{cases}u_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}^{\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}}}(x,t)&\text{if }t\in[0,50],\\
-u_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}^{\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}}}(x,100-t)&\text{if }t\in[50,100].\end{cases}

u_{n}\in L^{\infty}L^{\infty}
is then bounded by
1
.

Let
f_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, then for
f^{\mathcal{K}_{n}}
given by Proposition
6.6
, we have the following Lagrangian solution
f^{n}\in C^{0}L^{1}
to (
TE
) along
u_{n}
with initial data
f_{0}
,

(71)

f^{n}(x,t)=\begin{cases}f^{\mathcal{K}_{n}}(x,t)&\text{if }t\in[0,50],\\
f^{\mathcal{K}_{n}}(x,100-t)&\text{if }t\in[50,100].\end{cases}

We now apply Theorem
4.3
. Let
d_{*}
be a metric inducing the weak-
*
topology on

\left\{u\in L^{\infty}\left([0,100];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})\right):\left\|u\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}}\leq 1\right\}.

Let
f_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, and denote for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\nu>0
, by
f^{n,\nu}
, respectively
f^{n}
, the unique weak solution to (

ν

-ADE
), respectively (
TE
), along
u_{n}
with initial data
f_{0}
. Moreover denote by
f^{\infty,\nu}
the unique weak solution to (

ν

-ADE
) along
u_{\infty}^{\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}}}
with initial data
f_{0}
.

Then by Theorem
4.3
,

S.1

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
\nu_{n}>0
,
\epsilon_{n}>0
depending only on
\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}}
(and in particular not on
f_{0}
), with
\nu_{n}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0
monotonically, such that the following hold true:

S.2

For all
p\in[1,\infty)

\sup_{\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|f^{n,\nu}-f^{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0,

S.4

If
d_{*}(u_{n+1},u_{n})\leq\epsilon_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then for all
p\in[1,\infty)

\sup_{\nu_{n}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{1}}\left\|f^{n,\nu}-f^{\infty,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

We now choose
\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}}
. Proceeding by induction on
N\in\mathbb{N}
, assume there exists a sequence
\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{K}_{N}}}\subset\mathbb{N}
, so that with
\{u_{n}\}_{n=1}^{N}\subset L^{\infty}([0,100];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2}))
given by (
70
), and
\{\epsilon_{n}\}_{n=1}^{N}\subset(0,\infty)
given by (
S.1
), we have that for all
n\in\{1,...,N-1\}
,

d_{*}(u_{n+1},u_{n})\leq\epsilon_{n}.

We next pick
L_{\mathcal{K}_{N+1}}\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that for any
L_{\mathcal{K}_{N+1}}\in\mathbb{N}
we obtain by (
70
) a vector field
u_{N+1}
.

By (
46
) and Definition
6.5
, we see that as
L_{\mathcal{K}_{N+1}}\to\infty
,

d_{*}(u_{N+1},u_{N})\xrightarrow{(L_{\mathcal{K}_{N+1}})\to\infty}0,

and so we may pick
L_{\mathcal{K}_{N+1}}
large enough that
d_{*}(u_{N+1},u_{n})\leq\epsilon_{N}
. This completes the inductive step.

That is, there exists a sequence
\{L_{\mathcal{P}}\}_{\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{N}}\subset\mathbb{N}
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, with
u_{n}
given by (
70
), we have that
d_{*}(u_{n+1},u_{n})\leq\epsilon_{n}
. Therefore, (
S.2
), and (
S.4
) are satisfied.

Next, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and for all
p\in[1,\infty)
, we see that

\displaystyle\sup_{\nu_{n+1}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|f^{\infty,\nu}-f^{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\nu_{n+1}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|f^{\infty,\nu}-f^{n+1,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\nu_{n+1}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\nu_{n+1}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|f^{n,\nu}-f^{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}},

Therefore, by (
S.2
), (
S.4
), if for all
p\in[1,\infty)
,

(72)

\sup_{\nu_{n+1}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0,

then also for all
p\in[1,\infty)
,

\sup_{\nu_{n+1}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|f^{\infty,\nu}-f^{n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

Therefore, the statement of Theorem
6.7
follows from Proposition
6.6
applied to (
71
).

Notice that for all
p\in(1,\infty)
, (
72
) follows from the case
p=1
by interpolation with the existing uniform bound in
L^{\infty}L^{\infty}
. We, therefore, only prove (
72
) for
p=1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Express
\mathcal{K}_{n+1}\in\mathcal{N}
as
\mathcal{K}_{n+1}=(k,m,i,q)
with
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
m\in\{1,...,k\}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, and
q\in\{1,...,2^{\left\lfloor k/2\right\rfloor}\}
.

Then, by the expression (
70
), and Definitions
6.4
,
6.5
, we have that for all
t\notin[T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}},T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k}]\cup[100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}]
, that
u_{n+1}(\cdot,t)=u_{n}(\cdot,t)
.

Therefore, for all
\nu>0
,
f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\in C^{0}L^{1}
is a solution to (

ν

-ADE
) on the time interval
[0,T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}]
along the same
u_{n}
with initial data
0\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.

Similarly, on the time interval
[T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k}]
with initial data
(f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu})(\cdot,T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.

Similarly, on the time interval
[100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}},100]
with initial data
(f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu})(\cdot,100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.

Applying the
L^{p}
-Inequality (
7
) to these three cases shows that

(73)

\displaystyle\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}=0,

(74)

\displaystyle\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k}];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}\leq\left\|\left(f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right)(\cdot,T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k})\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})},

\displaystyle\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}},100];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}\leq\left\|\left(f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right)(\cdot,100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}})\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}.

Therefore, using the continuity
f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\in C^{0}L^{1}
, we have the bound

(75)

\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{1}}\\
\leq\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}},T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k}]\cap[100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}.

Next, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
given in terms of
n\in\mathbb{N}
by the expression
\mathcal{K}_{n+1}=(k,m,i,q)
, we aim to prove the bound, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and for all
\nu>0
,

(76)

\begin{gathered}\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}},T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k}];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}\\
\leq 2\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}2^{-\lfloor k/2\rfloor}+C\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{\nu 2^{-k}},\end{gathered}

with
C>0
independent of
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\nu>0
.

In the expression
\mathcal{K}_{n+1}=(k,m,i,q)
, we assume, without loss of generality, that
i=1
. The case
i=2
then follows the same argument with the coordinates reversed.

Let
J=[(q-1)2^{-\left\lfloor k/2\right\rfloor},q2^{-\left\lfloor k/2\right\rfloor}]\subset\mathbb{T}
.

Then, by (
45
), for all
t\in[0,3\cdot 2^{-k}]
, and for all
x\notin J\times\mathbb{T}
, we see that the binary swap vector field
u^{(i,k,q,L_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}})}(x,t)=0
.

Therefore, for all
t\in[T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}},T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k}]\cap[100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}]
, and for all
x\notin J\times\mathbb{T}
, we have that
u_{n+1}(x,t),u_{n}(x,t)=0
.

We first tackle the case
t\in[T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}},T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k}]
.

Let
\Omega=[0,1-2^{-\lfloor k/2\rfloor}]\times\mathbb{T}\subset\mathbb{T}^{2}
, a periodic strip.

Then, on the spatio-temporal domain
((x_{1},x_{2}),t)\in\Omega\times[0,3\cdot 2^{-k}]
, we see that
(f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu})((x_{1}+q2^{-\lfloor k/2\rfloor},x_{2}),t+T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}})
is a solution to the heat equation, and by (
73
) has the initial data
0
. However, its boundary data is unknown, so we will construct a second solution to the heat equation on the same domain, with initial data 0, which is an upper bound on the boundary, and then apply the maximum principle.

We have the a priori bound
\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}}\leq 2\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}
.

Therefore, we may apply hypo-ellipticity for the heat equation (see for example Section 4.4 in
[
31
]
) to deduce that
(f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu})((x_{1}+q2^{-\lfloor k/2\rfloor},x_{2}),t+T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}})
is smooth on the interior of the domain
\Omega\times[0,3\cdot 2^{-k}]
.

We introduce

\displaystyle\mathrm{erf}(x)=\int_{-\infty}^{x}e^{-y^{2}}\;dy,

\displaystyle C_{0}=\mathrm{erf}\left(0\right),

\displaystyle a=1-2^{-\lfloor k/2\rfloor}.

We define the following solution to the heat equation.

\displaystyle\theta:\Omega\times[0,3\cdot 2^{-k}]\to\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle\theta((x_{1},x_{2}),t)=2\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}C_{0}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{-x_{1}}{\sqrt{4\nu t}}\right)+\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{x_{1}-a}{\sqrt{4\nu t}}\right)\right).

Observe that
\theta
has initial data
0
, and its value on the boundary
\partial\Omega=\{0,a\}\times\mathbb{T}
is greater than or equal to
2\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}
. Also for all
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\Omega
, we have that
\theta((x_{1},x_{2}),t)
is an increasing function of
t\in[0,3\cdot 2^{-k}]
.

Therefore, by maximum principle, we have the following point-wise bound on the interior of the domain, for all
(x_{1},x_{2})\in(0,1-2^{-\lfloor k/2\rfloor})\times\mathbb{T}
, and for all
t\in(0,3\cdot 2^{-k})
,

\left|(f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu})((x_{1}+q2^{-\lfloor k/2\rfloor},x_{2}),t+T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}})\right|\leq\theta((x_{1},x_{2}),3\cdot 2^{-k}).

Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}},T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k}];L^{1}((\mathbb{T}\setminus J)\times\mathbb{T}))}

\displaystyle\leq 4\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}C_{0}^{-1}\int_{0}^{a}\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{-x}{\sqrt{4\nu\cdot 3\cdot 2^{-k}}}\right)\;dx.

After changing variables, for
C=8\sqrt{3}C_{0}^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{erf}(-x)\;dx
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}},T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k}];L^{1}((\mathbb{T}\setminus J)\times\mathbb{T}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{\nu 2^{-k}}.

Since additionally
J\times\mathbb{T}
has Lebesgue-measure
2^{-\lfloor k/2\rfloor}
, and we have the a priori bound
\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{\infty}}\leq 2\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}
, we deduce (
76
).

Next, since
k
in determined by
n
through the map
n\mapsto\mathcal{K}_{n+1}=(k,i,m,q)
, denote this now by
k_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
.

Since
n\mapsto\mathcal{K}_{n+1}
respects the well-orders
(\mathbb{N},<)
,
(\mathcal{N},<_{\mathrm{lex}})
, we see that
k_{n}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\infty
. Moreover, by (
S.1
), we have that
\nu_{n}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0
.

Therefore, by (
76
), we see that

(77)

\sup_{\nu_{n+1}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}},T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k_{n}}];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

By (
75
), (
77
), the proof of (
72
) will then be complete if also

(78)

\sup_{\nu_{n+1}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k_{n}},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

To this end denote by
g^{n,\nu}\in C^{0}([100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k_{n}},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}],L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
the solution to (

ν

-ADE
) along
u_{n}
on the time interval
[100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k_{n}},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}]
with initial data
f^{n+1,\nu}(\cdot,100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k_{n}})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.

Then, arguing as in (
76
), we have the same bound. For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
\nu>0
,

\begin{gathered}\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-g^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k_{n}},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}\\
\leq 2\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}2^{-\lfloor k_{n}/2\rfloor}+C\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\sqrt{\nu 2^{-k_{n}}}.\end{gathered}

Therefore, as before, we deduce that

(79)

\sup_{\nu_{n+1}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|f^{n+1,\nu}-g^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k_{n}},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

By the
L^{p}
-Inequality (
7
), and (
74
), we have that, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and for all
\nu>0
,

\displaystyle\left\|g^{n,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k_{n}},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(f^{n+1,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right)(\cdot,T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}+3\cdot 2^{-k_{n}})\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}.

So, by (
77
),

\sup_{\nu_{n+1}\leq\nu\leq\nu_{n}}\left\|g^{n,\nu}-f^{n,\nu}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}-3\cdot 2^{-k_{n}},100-T_{\mathcal{K}_{n+1}}];L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

Therefore, by also (
79
), we deduce (
78
).
∎
